115年 10月賦稅收入初步統計 
一、整體賦稅收入 
（一）實徵淨額 
10 月實徵淨額 5,669 億元，較上年同月增加 1,495 億元；累計 1 至
10 月實徵 4 兆 1,383 億元，較上年同期增加 8,499 億元(+25.8%)，
主要由證交稅、營所稅及營業稅所貢獻。隨新版財政收支劃分法實
施，營業稅劃歸中央政府比率由 61.2%降為 4.5%(獲配稅額隨之減
少 2,662 億元)，致中央政府稅收增加數 3,906 億元(+15.8%)，不及
地方政府增加數 4,671 億元(+68.5%)。 
（二）預算達成率 
累 計1 至 10 月 實 徵 淨 額 占 全 年 預 算 數 91.4% ， 其 中 中 央 政 府 為
92.8%，地方政府為87.7%；與全年預算數差距分別為3,913億元、
2,227億元、1,614億元。 
圖 1 賦稅實徵淨額及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）10 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營利事業所得稅增加 1,133 億元、
證券交易稅增加 220 億元及綜合所得稅增加 120 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,297
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 2,986 億元及營業稅增加 1,272 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化

主因暫繳稅款成長。

億元(+26.7%)，主因結算申報自繳、暫繳稅款俱增及延分期稅款
陸續入帳。

扣繳稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 8,894 億元，較上年同

配、薪資所得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。

4.貨物稅
10月實徵淨額73億元，較上年同月減少41億元(-35.6%)，累計1至10
月實徵淨額948億元，較上年同期減少248億元(-20.7%)，主因新購汽
機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增加，加以汽柴油減徵幅度擴大所致。
5.證券交易稅
(1)10 月及累計 1 至 10 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 1,210
億元、1 兆 1,437 億元，分別較上年同期(6,267 億元、4,632 億元)
增 78.9%、1.5 倍。
(2)10 月證交稅實徵淨額 523 億元， 較上年同月增加 220 億元
(+72.6%)；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 5,583 億元，較上年同期增加
3,297 億元(+1.4 倍)。
6.關稅
10 月實徵淨額 132 億元，較上年同月增加 7 億元(+6.0%)，累計 1 至
10 月實徵淨額 1,356 億元，較上年同期增加 88 億元(+6.9%)，均因機
械及電機設備、基本金屬、塑橡膠及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
10 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月減少 11 億元(-31.9%)，主因大額

稅款案件減少；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 234 億元，較上年同期增加
32 億元(+15.9%)，主因大額稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
10 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+5.9%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 560 億元，較上年同期減少
11 億元(-2.0%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.使用牌照稅
10 月實徵淨額 6 億元，較上年同月減少 1 億元(-10.2%)，累計 1 至
10 月實徵淨額 676 億元，較上年同期減少 1 億元(-0.1%)，主因開徵
輛數減少，以及稅款入帳時間差異所致。
（三）前 10月主要稅目實徵淨額占全年預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%(已提前達成全年預算目標)者有遺產稅(123%)、贈與稅
(107%)、印花稅(106%)、房屋稅(105%)及營所稅(102%)。
2.低於 100%者有牌照稅(99%)、營業稅(94%)、綜所稅(93%)、契稅
(90%)、關稅(88%)、菸酒稅(81%)、證交稅(80%)、貨物稅(79%)、土

增稅(71%)及特銷稅(47%)等。

Document 022: 2014年GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ ～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2014年GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ ～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026年 11月 6日

1/7 
2014年 GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ 
～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ GPIFが基本ポートフォリオを変更し、国内債券の割合を引き上げるのではないかとの観測が
市場で浮き沈みしている。本稿では、現在の基本ポートフォリオの起点となった 2014年の運
用改革の成立過程を分析し、変更する場合にどのようなことが起き得るのか考察する。 
○ 2014年改革では、基本ポートフォリオに占める国内債券を60％から 35％へ大幅に引き下げ、
国内外の株式を約 2倍に増やした。アベノミクスの象徴の一つとして GPIF改革は語られる
ことが多いが、改革につながる議論は民主党政権期から始まっていた。 
○ 当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事録によれば、株式をどこまで増やすかより、国内債券をどこま
で減らすかが議論の中心だった。また、当時決定した国内債券 35％という配分は、期待収益
率やリスクだけで機械的に決まったものではなく、移行コストや市場への影響などを考慮し
て決定された。市場への影響を抑えるため、正式決定前から乖離許容幅を使って国内債券を
減らしていたのは、今後の動向を見極めるうえで興味深い。当面、四半期の運用実績に注目
が集まるだろう。 
○ 足元では長期金利が上昇し、低金利が懸念点だった 2014年当時とは逆の環境にある。当時の
運用委員会では、金利が上がれば国内債券の期待収益率が高まり、最適化をやり直すことで
国債の割合が上昇し得るとの説明がなされていた。当時の考え方を踏まえれば、現在の基本
ポートフォリオが「金利のある世界」でも最適なのか再考の余地があろう。

### Page 2

2/7 
1. はじめに 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）を巡り、基本ポートフォリオの見直し観測が市場で
浮き沈みしている。きっかけは片山さつき財務・金融担当相が2026年7月、GPIFをはじめとする
年金基金について、日本の金融資産への投資をさらに増やす方向を後押ししたいとの考えを示し
たことだ。長期金利が上昇するなかでの発言で、日本の金融資産とは特に国内債券だと解されて
いる。こうしたなか、GPIF側では同年 8月の経営委員会の議事次第に「基本ポートフォリオ検証
等PTにおける議論について」が報告事項に入り、経営委員会への関心が高まった。 
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは 5年に 1度、公的年金の財政検証を踏まえて見直す。現在の基本
ポートフォリオは2025年度が開始年だ。制度上、毎年度検証し、必要があれば 5年間の中期目標
期間中でも見直しを検討することになっているものの、GPIF発足後、中期目標期間の途中で基本
ポートフォリオを変更したのは 2013年 6月と 2014年 10月の 2回にとどまる。 
2014年10月の見直しでは、国内債券比率を 60％から 35％へ引き下げ、国内外の株式をそれぞ
れ12％から25％へ引き上げる大幅な変更が行われた。株式比率の上昇によって損失が大きくなる
リスクを懸念する向きはあったが、運用損失だけでなく、年金財政上、目標とする利回りを達成
できないこともリスクと捉える考え方を明確にした。2014年改革の本質は公的年金運用における
「安全」という概念を変えたことだと筆者はみている。 
では、仮にいま国内債券を再び増やすとなれば、改革の逆行を意味するのか。この問いを考え
るため、年金財政からの議論と成長戦略からの要請という二つの流れを民主党政権期からたど
り、2014年改革がどのように実現したのかを振り返る。

2. 民主党政権期――国債中心の「安全運用」への疑問 
2014年改革は第2次安倍晋三政権の経済政策「アベノミクス」の象徴の一つとして語られるこ
とが多い。しかし、その成立過程をさかのぼると、改革につながる議論は第 2次安倍政権の発足
前からあった。民主党政権期の議論では、公的年金の運用における「安全」を巡る議論が次第に
成熟していったことが確認できる。 
2006年に発足したGPIFの基本ポートフォリオは、当初国内債券が 67％を占めていた（資料 1）。
このうち、大半を占める国債は満期に額面で償還される。国債偏重の狙いは、厚生労働省の検討
会が 2010年12月にまとめた報告書にも表れている。公的年金運用の考え方として、保険料は
「投資を目的として徴収されたもの」ではないとして、「安全運用が基本」とした（注 1）。検討会
では、全額を国債で運用すべきだとの意見もあった。

### Page 3

3/7 
資料 1 GPIFの基本ポートフォリオの変遷

（注）2014年 10月以降は短期資産を基本ポートフォリオに含めない。 
（出所）GPIFの公表資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

もっとも、国債を多く持つことが本当に安全なのかという疑問はすでにこの検討会で示されて
いた。運用上のリスクを単なる損失だけで捉えず、年金財政上予定された収益を下回り、将来の
保険料率の引き上げや給付の引き下げにつながる可能性まで考慮する必要があるとしている。国
債を多く持てば安全だという単純な整理には収まらない議論が存在していたことが確認できる。 
この検討会に続く社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会では、国債への集中はリスクになると
いう考えが強く示されるようになる。2012年 2月に同委員会の議論を整理した資料（注2）では
国債のみの運用は一つの資産への集中であり、分散投資ができていないという意味でリスクが非
常に大きい、という指摘が確認できる。デフレと低金利がこの先も続くとは考えにくく、物価や
賃金が上昇すれば、給付する年金額は増える一方で債券価格は下落し、損失を被る可能性が高い
というアベノミクス下での改革を先取りするような意見も出た。国債のみの運用で、将来にわた
り物価や賃金の上昇率を上回る利回りを確保できるのか疑問が残る、との意見もあった。 
民主党政権後期の議論は、短期の運用損失を過度に重視するのではなく、国債偏重によって年
金財政上、必要な利回りを確保できないリスクを考慮する方向へと発展していった。

3. 産業競争力会議――成長戦略からの見直し要請 
GPIF改革に至る過程では、日本経済の成長戦略の文脈で国債偏重の見直しを求める意見があっ
た。これは安倍政権時だけでなく民主党政権期にもあった。国内債券を減らすべきだという方向
は、年金財政からの議論と重なる。ただ、見直しを求める理由は両政権期とも公的年金の巨額の
資金を経済成長に生かすという発想から来ている。 
安倍政権でGPIF改革の舞台となったのは、政権発足直後の2013年 1月に始まった産業競争力会
議だ。「公的・準公的資金の有効活用」が初回から議論にのぼり、安全資産に偏った年金などの運
用を見直し、成長分野への資金供給につなげる考え方が示された。同会議が進むなかでは、民間

### Page 4

4/7 
議員から「アクティブ運用をし、そこに中長期でもの言う株主ということで企業が新陳代謝も進
めるようなツールとしていく」といった発言が飛び出している（注 3）。 GPIFの資金を企業改革や
経済成長に生かそうとする考え方であり、「専ら被保険者の利益」のために運用するという年金積
立金の原則との緊張をはらんでいた。

4. 有識者会議――成長戦略と被保険者利益の両立 
成長戦略のなかで出たこうした要請を、公的年金の運用原則とどのように両立させたのだろう
か。この問題に対応したのが「公的・準公的資金の運用・リスク管理等の高度化等に関する有識
者会議」だった。この会議は政府の日本再興戦略を受けて経済再生担当相の下に設置され、GPIF
改革の理論的柱を打ち立てた。 
公的年金の運用に国民が何を最も期待しているのか明確に定義した点がこの会議のポイントだ
ろう。2013年 11月の報告書は、「現役世代の保険料負担を維持しつつ、将来の年金給付に支障が
生じないよう、年金財政の長期的な健全性の確保に資すること」こそが国民の最も期待している
ことだとした（注4）。目標とする運用利回りが達成できなければ、保険料の引き上げか将来の年
金給付水準の引き下げが迫られる可能性が生じる。 
この定義に則り、デフレから脱却して適度なインフレ環境へ移るというアベノミクスの目指し
た環境でのポートフォリオのあり方を検討した。安全資産とされる国債を多く持てば安全だと考
えるのではなく、年金財政に必要な収益を確保できるかという観点から基本ポートフォリオを考
える方向が明確に示された。 
収益率の向上という文脈のなかで、産業競争力会議で出たようなGPIFの資金を企業改革に生か
すというアイデアは運用改革に取り込まれていく。投資先企業との対話や議決権行使を通じて企
業価値の向上を促し、その成果を運用収益として享受するという考え方が報告書で示された。成
長戦略が期待した企業改革を、被保険者の利益につながる運用につなげたのだ。 
社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会もこれに呼応する形で、デフレから脱却して適度なイン
フレ環境へ移る状況では、あらかじめ「国内債券中心」と示す必要はなく、GPIFが運用目的・目
標に沿ったポートフォリオを検討すべきだとした。年金積立金の運用目標は、年金給付費が長期
的には名目賃金上昇率に連動することから、賃金上昇率をどの程度上回るかで設定される。同専
門委員会は、財政検証に向けて検討した複数の経済前提を踏まえ、GPIFが確保すべき運用利回り
として、名目賃金上昇率を 1.7％上回る水準を示した（注5）。

5. GPIF運用委員会――国内債券 35％の決定過程 
ここから2014年 10月の基本ポートフォリオ変更に至る議論を、当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事
録からたどる。2014年改革は、株式を増やした改革とみられがちだ。しかし、運用委員会の議論
の中心になったのは株式の比率ではなく、国内債券の扱いだった。後年公表された議事録を読む
と、国内債券中心では目標の利回りが達成できないどころか、金利が上昇した場合には多額の損
失を被るといった懸念が、当時の事務局や理事長から示されている。当時の長期金利は 0.5％前後

### Page 5

5/7 
と、歴史的な低水準にあった（資料 2）。事務局の試算も、国内債券の比率を 45％以下に減らさな
ければ目標の利回りに届かない、という形で示された。

資料 2 日本国債 10年物利回り

（注）10年物国債利回りの月末値。2026年 11月は 6日の値。 
（出所）Bloombergより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

GPIF内部の議論では株式をどこまで増やすかではなく、国内債券をどこまで減らすかが議論の
焦点だったことが分かる。運用委員会の委員長は最終段階で、「国債を減らしたというのが一番の
主で、その結果として、こういうポートフォリオが書けた」と振り返っているのが象徴的だろう
（注 6）。 
基本ポートフォリオの決定に至る議論で興味深いのは、GPIFの売買が国内市場に与える影響を
相当程度考慮に入れていた点だ。事務局から示された基本ポートフォリオ候補は当初、五つあっ
た。このなかには、現在のポートフォリオである 4資産を 25％ずつとする案も含まれていた。こ
の案は、現状からの移行を考えると「現実感がゼロ」とされ、早い段階で検証の対象から外れた
（注 7）。事務局は候補を絞り込む際に足元の資産構成からの距離を物差しに加え、最終案の選定
理由にも「足下の資産構成と相対的に近く、移行に係るコストが小さい」ことを挙げている（注
8）。最終段階では、1％刻みで確認すると、経済中位ケースでは国内債券 34％などのより効率的な
配分も見つかった。それでも、国内債券をさらに減らして国内株式を増やせば移行の影響が大き
くなるとして、35％が維持された。外国市場に比べ、国内市場ではGPIFの売買の影響が大きいと
いう判断が働いていた。 
国内債券の売却は、正式な決定を待たずに始まっていた。運用委員会は2014年4月の時点で、
現行の基本ポートフォリオが認める範囲の中で国内債券を減らす方針を了承している。委員長は

### Page 6

6/7 
その目的を、金利上昇に備えて「債券の評価損を抑える」ことだと確認した（注9）。8月には、
その範囲を超えて国内債券を売り、ほかの資産に振り向ける方針も了承された。事務局は、積立
金規模が巨額であるため、市場インパクトやフロントランニングを誘発せずに速やかに資産配分
を変更することは現実的ではないと説明し、基本ポートフォリオの正式決定前から資産配分の変
更を検討する考えを示した（注 10）。正式決定に先立つ 8月末には、国内債券の比率はすでに
50％程度まで下がっていた。 
GPIFの現在の中期目標でも、市場の価格形成や民間の投資行動等を歪めないことが求められて
いる。今後、基本ポートフォリオを変更する場合にも、市場への影響を抑えるため、正式な変更
に先立って資産配分を調整することが考えられる。今回も運用の動向を見極めるうえで、四半期
の運用実績が注目される。

6. おわりに 
2014年にGPIFが中期目標期間の途中で基本ポートフォリオを変更した背景には、先行きの運用
環境の変化を見通した判断があった。GPIFは当時、日本経済が「長年続いたデフレからの転換と
いう大きな運用環境の変化の節目」にあるとし、「長期的な経済環境の変化に速やかに対応する観
点から、来年度を待たず」に変更に踏み切った（注 11）。実際には低金利がその後も長く続いた
が、足元では当時懸念されていた金利上昇が現実のものとなっている。 
長期金利は2026年 9月に 3％台に達し、約 30年ぶりの水準を付けた。奇しくも3％という数字
は、2014年のGPIF運用委員会でもたびたび登場していた。当時の理事長は、国内債券 60％という
従来の基本ポートフォリオについて、「国内債券の利回りが 3％というものを大前提としてつくっ
ている」と述べていた（注 12）。  
興味深いのは、2014年の運用委員会で、金利が上昇すれば国内債券の将来の期待収益率が高ま
り、改めて資産配分の最適化を行うことで「自然に国債の割合がまたもとに戻るというか、上が
っていく」とGPIFの事務局側が説明していたことだ（注 13）。 2020年の基本ポートフォリオ変更
では、国内金利の低下を踏まえ、国内債券を減らして外国債券を増やした。今後は、長期の収益
確保を担う株式の比率を維持しつつ、為替リスクなどを考慮して外国債券の一部を国内債券に振
り向けることも選択肢として考えられる。2014年に国内債券を減らしたのと同じ考え方が、今度
はその割合を引き上げる判断につながり得るのではないだろうか。

日本：個人消費は所得の伸びを下回る緩慢な拡大。先行き不安が回復の重石に
5
 個人消費は拡大基調を維持も、所得の伸びを下回る緩慢な拡大
―9月の消費活動指数は前年比＋0.8％と拡大基調を維持したものの、耐久財特需のはく落などから増勢は鈍化
― 実質雇用者報酬が着実に増加する一方、消費の伸びは所得を下回る状況が継続
 先行き不安も家計の慎重な支出姿勢に影響
― 消費者マインドは食品やエネルギーなど身近な消費財の価格上昇に対する懸念から低調に推移
― 中東情勢を巡る不透明感もあり、当面の消費は緩慢な拡大にとどまる見通し

 一方、DC増設に伴う将来の電気料金負担増や水資源への影響を懸念し、規制強化を求める動きが拡大
― 中間選挙では、DC規制の強化を掲げる民主党知事候補が各州で勝利。とりわけ、DCの集積が進むテキサス・オハイ
オ・ジョージアでは、長年共和党が知事職を握ってきたものの、民主党候補が勝利する結果。今後、DC開発を巡る規制

日本：7～9月期実質GDPは横ばい圏の推移。消費低調の一方、設備投資が堅調に回復

（年／月）
日本：7月以降の原油高等を受けて輸入物価が再加速。一部の企業間取引価格にも波及の動き
14
 10月の輸入物価は、前年比＋24.8％（前月：同＋23.8％）と再加速
―7月以降の原油高再燃に加え、タンカー運賃の上昇影響もあり、石油・石炭・天然ガスが加速
―AI・データセンター需要等を背景に、半導体等の電気・電子機器や銅関連を含む金属・同製品も引き続き高い伸び率
 輸入物価の再加速は、国内企業物価の石油・化学関連製品に波及。今後、消費者向け製品の価格にも転嫁へ

― 原油高再燃等の影響が、国内企業物価の川上から川中段階の石油・化学関連製品へ速いペースで波及
― 今後、家事用消耗品などの消費者段階へと価格転嫁が進み、消費者物価の押し上げ圧力になる見込み

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
4Q26 iPhone builds unchanged: We maintain our 4Q26 iPhone build forecast at
72M units (+33% q/q, -5% y/y). While builds are expected to increase sequentially,
volumes remain below last year’s level, with softer legacy-model production,
including the iPhone 16/17e, weighing on the outlook. Our 2H26 build forecast for

the new iPhone 18 family remains unchanged at 71M units (-21% y/y), with the y/y
comparison also affected by one fewer SKU launched than last year. Within this, we
continue to expect 7M units of iPhone Duo builds. We will continue to monitor the
production ramp and sell-through data for potential upside or downside to our build
estimates. Our full-year forecast implies CY26 iPhone shipments of 234M units
(+3% y/y), with modest growth for the year despite the softer 4Q y/y outlook.

4Q26 iPad builds unchanged; year-end production outlook remains subdued: We
maintain our 4Q26 iPad build forecast at 10.3M units (-10% q/q, -26% y/y), implying
continued pressure on production volumes into year-end. Our full-year estimate
remains 46.8M units (-14% y/y), with the sharper y/y contraction in 4Q pointing to a
weaker finish to the year.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

公司簡介 
上品為從事氟素樹脂材料相關製品之生產
廠商，主要經營氟素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相
關製品，包括氟素原料加工之半成品及成品
生產及銷售，下游應用廣泛，是全球氟素樹
脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段
設備、製造、研發、創新、設計等完整服務
的企業。3Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設
備營收占 69.7% ， 氟 素 內 襯 管 件 類 占
10.2%，氟素應用材料類占 16.5 %，氟素
工程收入占 3.7%。3Q26 公司營收按地區
別區分，台灣占 47.4%，中國占 48.2%，美
國占 0.5%，其他亞洲占 1.6%，歐洲占
2.2%。

主要客戶： 
主要競爭對手：

葉家宏 bill.yeh@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

上品 (4770 TT) 
在手訂單金額提升，持續擴充產能因應

永豐觀點 
上品在手訂單金額提升至 80 億元，隨著先前低毛利訂單陸續消化完畢，產品組合
預期改善，公司規劃於 2027 持續進行產能擴充，後市展望持續正向，研究部維持
正向看待上品獲利表現。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議維持買進，主要係因：上品在手訂單金額持續提升至 80 億元，反映台灣
及中國地區接單表現持續暢旺，隨著公司低毛利訂單出貨完畢，產品組合應逐步好
轉，且公司規劃於 2027 持續擴充產能，挹注未來營收動能，研究部預估公司
2026/2027 稅後 EPS 14.55/23.01 元，參考歷史 PER 區間約位於 10-20X，以
2027 年預估 EPS，給予 15X PER 評價，目標價 345 元，投資建議維持買進。

ESG 評析 
上品企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2024 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學)

專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。

### Page 2

2

上品為從事氟素樹脂材料相關製品之生產廠商：公司成立於 1981 年，主要經營氟
素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相關製品，包括氟素原料加工之半成品及成品生產及銷售，
下游應用廣泛，是全球氟素樹脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段設備、製
造、研發、創新、設計等完整服務的企業。3Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設備營
收占 69.7%，氟素內襯管件類占 10.2%，氟素應用材料類占 16.5 %，氟素工程收
入占 3.7%；3Q26 公司產品依應用類別區分，半導體占營收 95.1%，化工應用占
比為 3.1%，面板及太陽能應用占 1.4%，其他應用占 0.4%，3Q26 公司營收按地
區別區分，台灣占 47.4%，中國占 48.2%，美國占 0.5%，其他亞洲占 1.6%，歐
洲占 2.2%。 
3Q26 EPS 4.27 元，優於預期：上品 3Q26 營收 16 億元（+22.5% QoQ，+14.4% 
YoY），優於預期，主因上品於 2Q26 擴產，並持續拉升產能利用率，推升營收表
現，毛利率 34.64%（-1.91pts. QoQ，+4.91pts. YoY），低於預期，主因上品出
貨較多低毛利訂單，影響產品組合所致，稅後淨利 34.1 億元（+37.2% QoQ，
+39.9% YoY），優於預期，其中，營業費用低於預期，主因認列預期信用損失金
額降低所致，3Q26 稅後 EPS 4.27 元，優於預期。

### Page 3

3

在手訂單金額持續提升：上品在手訂單金額達 80 億元，較前次發報告時 75 億元
持續提升，其中，約 75%為彰濱廠訂單，約 25%為嘉興廠訂單，預期到年底在手
訂單金額將持續再提升。2026 接單槽車數量達 1,100 台，反映台灣化學品廠因應
晶圓廠大客戶需求而擴充產能，對於上品內襯設備及內襯管件需求同步增溫，而中
國記憶體廠及化學品廠亦持續擴充，下單動能有所增溫。隨著市況於 2026 大幅改
善，彰濱廠及嘉興廠已可挑選毛利率較佳的訂單進行承接，產品組合預期將逐步好
轉，有利其毛利率逐步回升。 
2027 上品計畫持續擴充產能：因應客戶需求強勁，公司於 2Q26 進行擴產，並持
續招工補充人力，拉升產能利用率，目前彰濱廠及嘉興廠已達近滿載水準，公司年
初彰濱廠槽車約當產能約為月產能 80 台，近期已提升至月產能 130 台，預期年底
將達滿載規模月產能 140 台，公司規劃將於 6M27 再擴充至月產能 180 台，嘉興
廠亦規劃提升產能約 30%，以因應客戶強勁需求。而先前承接的低毛利訂單仍有
部分尚未出貨完畢，預期將於 1Q27 出貨完畢，因營收比重持續下降，預期產品組
合應持續改善，有利毛利率及獲利表現回升。

後市展望正向，投資建議維持買進：展望後市，台灣化學品廠及中國記憶體、化學
品廠擴建，推升對上品氟素樹脂產品需求，上品在手訂單金額持續提升，隨著上品
先前承接低毛利訂單陸續出貨完畢，產品組合應逐步改善，推升毛利率及獲利表現
回升。上品規劃於 2027 持續進行產能擴充，挹注未來營收動能，且已可挑選毛利
率較佳的訂單進行承接，後市獲利展望正向。研究部預估公司 2026 營收 56.9 億
元（+20.3% YoY），毛利率 35.1%，稅後淨利 11.6 億元（+41.6% YoY），稅後
EPS 14.55 元，預估 2027 營收 76.8 億元（+35.1% YoY），毛利率 38.31%，稅
後淨利 18.4 億元（+59.2% YoY），稅後 EPS 23.01 元，參考歷史 PER 區間約位
於 10-20X，以 2027 年預估 EPS，給予 15X PER 評價，目標價 345 元，維持買進
投資建議。

研 究 報 告                
旺矽(6223.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
探針卡需求強勁挹注短期營運動能強勁、CPO 設備進度如期  
 投資評價與建議 
目前探針卡廠商評價普遍修正至20~35倍，尤其以短期營運相對穩健之廠商
(如精測、旺矽)仍相對有撐，而我們觀察公司探針卡需求強勁支撐短期營運動
能強勁，並重申CPO設備驗證進度如期、客戶數持續增加，故仍看好評價仍有
望回到合理區間之中上緣。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率
維持近3Q26之高檔水準，觀察探針卡BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，且擴產
計畫不變。基於公司10月營收達22.3億元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，
預估營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年：
(1)VPC和MEMS明年底有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，擴充幅度仍具有上
修空間，當前VPC主要客戶未有升級至MEMS規劃，至於MEMS主要貢獻
則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU；(2)insertion 2客戶將於12月通知驗證結
果，1H27有望獲得量產機訂單，至於insertion 3已如期出貨工程機給3間
客戶，並預期1H27將陸續獲得量產機訂單，惟公司預估CPO設備營收顯
著貢獻時間點將落在2028年，故我們同步調整營收、獲利。 
 投資風險 
CPO進展不如預期、探針卡擴產針數下修。

| 營運概況 
3Q26獲利優於預期：3Q26營收季增12.8%至59億元，連續七季創下歷史新高，毛利率61.7%，稅後EPS 19.18元，
其中營收、獲利均優於預期，推估主因為探針卡產品組合改善，加上於2Q26進行之探針卡報價調整完整貢獻3Q
營收(包含CPC漲價20%、取消VPC和MEMS 10%價格優惠)，另公司營業費用管控得宜也同步挹注獲利。 
估4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率有望維持近3Q26之高檔水準，我們
推估動能包含：(1)維持VPC、MEMS月產能今年底分別擴至200萬針、350萬針之進展；(2)訂單出貨則觀察探針卡
BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，其中CPC今年前10個月維持滿載、目前BB Ratio接近1，至於MEMS、VPC則超過1，
考量公司探針卡交期已明顯延⻑至4~6個月，隱含本季探針卡營收仍有望持續提升。基於公司10月營收達22.3億
元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，預估本季營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年，我們觀察：(1)探針卡中，明年CPC月產能維
持50萬針、VPC和MEMS明年底則有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，產能擴充幅度具有上修空間，且強調2028年維持
積極擴產策略，訂單貢獻中，VPC主要客戶近年未有升級至MEMS探針卡之規劃，預期該客戶AI ASIC訂單將支撐
明年VPC需求，至於MEMS明年主要貢獻則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU，且公司持續爭取潛在客戶AI ASIC訂單，
另規劃於2H27量產之PCB廠有望挹注探針卡成本優化；(2)CPO設備中，insertion 2如我們先前所提，於3Q26底
～4Q26初送樣至客戶端進行驗證，公司預估客戶將於12月通知驗證結果，根據目前客戶回饋正向，預期1H27有
望獲得量產機訂單，此外近期有另⼀客戶與公司接洽中(公司預估1H27獲得驗證結果)，至於insertion 3則已如期
出貨工程機給3間客戶，也將陸續交機給另3間客戶，預計1H27將陸續獲得insertion 3量產機訂單，惟考量接單至
出貨、裝機驗收之時間較⻑，公司認為CPO相關設備顯著營收貢獻時間點將落在2028年，我們則同步下修明年相
關設備之營收認列數量。綜上所述，公司預估明年營收有望年增至少50%、毛利率維持在60%以上；我們則預估
明年營收有望年增58.6%至344.6億元，較先前下修主要反映部分CPO設備遞延至2028年認列營收，毛利率63.2%，
稅後EPS 117.77元。

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

한화에어로스페이스 (012450)

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -5,829 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Chỉ số VNINDEX

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1800 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1720 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1680 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

trưởng 6%-8%/năm

chiếm 50% thị phần doanh thu ngành bán lẻ dược phẩm, từ mức 17%-20% hiện tại

MACRO STRATEGY
Setting the Stage for 2027
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Typically, a new administration sets the stage in their first year with stimulative policy
changes that play out in the second year after the new policies have been enacted into
law and begin to have their impact. This pattern shows up in historical data with the
average rate of gross domestic product (GDP) growth highest in the second year of

presidential terms compared to the other three years of the presidential term. At the
same time, equity market returns tend to be lowest on average in the second year of
presidential terms, which seems to be at odds with the fact that growth is strongest.
This apparent inconsistency can be attributed to the fact that the stock market is a
leading indicator that anticipates stronger growth and higher profits well before they
actually arrive. Once they do, as they have in 2026, the markets are looking ahead to the

inevitable slowing from the policy-induced peak in growth that typically arrives after the
midterm election year. The midterms also create uncertainty about the subsequent policy
environment which damps stocks ahead of the election only to be reversed on average
once the elections are over and the outlook is clarified. This phenomenon is seen in the
fact that on average the stock market has corrected about 20% ahead of midterms only
to rise about 30% from that bottom in the year after.

Q3 GDP data illustrate this pattern. Economic growth picked up through the first three
quarters of 2026 to a peak of 3.4%. Consumer spending growth averaged 3.8% over the
past six months, while the AI investment boom accelerated at a midteens growth rate.
Private sector purchases were growing at just shy of a 5% growth rate. GDP growth is
lower than that because imports of Asia produced technology-gear for the AI buildout
are subtracting a good bit from that underlying 5% domestic demand growth pace.

The new policies aimed at helping consumers through lower taxes and investment
incentives to spur private sector production have boosted growth as expected. Still, this
acceleration is passing, and the fiscal boost is likely to fade in 2027. Profits naturally
have followed this stronger GDP growth higher.
The upward trend in analysts' earnings revisions has started to roll over in our view, and,
while earnings are still growing, the rate of growth is likely to slow from here. In simple

terms, the second derivative of growth has turned negative. From way above trend
toward trend growth is still sufficient for the bull market to continue.
Another aspect of this transition to midcycle aside from the fading midterm
election boost from fiscal measures is the shift of monetary policy from maximum
accommodation to a more neutral policy. The Fed's rate cuts helped the midterm
acceleration in growth. Its shift to removing accommodation with rate hikes this year

adds to the odds of some momentum loss in 2027. That's already apparent as Federal
Reserve (Fed) Chair Warsh noted in his press conference after the September Federal
Open Market Committee meeting. Weaker balance sheet borrowers have seen their
spreads blow out as credit becomes more expensive. So far, the damage is confined to
the lowest-quality borrowers. Before, these spreads were unusually low reflecting easy
money conditions that are now normalizing. It's still early days in the new credit cycle.

Of course, how the market responds to this new Fed tightening cycle depends on how
aggressively the Fed raises rates. Analysts at Ned Davis Research note the S&P 500
has gained an average of 4.4% in the first year of all postwar tightening cycles. This
outcome varies considerably depending on the extent of the tightening. Aggressive
tightening cycles have seen an average decline of 3.6% a year later, whereas gradual
tightenings have seen a 10.5% average gain. In short, as one might expect, too much

tightening can cause a decline in stocks. Currently, the market seems to anticipate a
Investment Implications
Equities should continue to outperform
bonds in 2027 in our view, but
performance is likely to shift in favor of
higher-quality, less leveraged segments
of the market as financial conditions
tighten and earnings momentum slows,
while the accelerating momentum of
the early cycle transitions to midcycle
cruise control.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
December and March nudge up in rates to reverse the easings by the Powell Fed. This
would be consistent with an ongoing equity bull market in 2027. Obviously, inflation
will have a lot to say about whether a mild or aggressive tightening cycle is called for.
With wage pressures benign, productivity strong and the early-cycle acceleration set to
peak, we believe the odds of an aggressive tightening cycle that derails the bull market

are low.
Other policy effects have shaped the current economic cycle. The shift toward more
nationalistic trade policies with tariffs, for example, has drastically impacted the pattern
of trade flows. U.S. imports from China have dropped from about 20% 10 years ago to
less than 10%, according to research from Strategas. Nevertheless, contrary to concerns
of a trade-induced recession, overall trade has continued to grow in line with the global
economy. Rather than deglobalization, the world is undergoing a reglobalization into new

trade spheres that better align with national interests.
According to McKinsey Quarterly, "The 2025 U.S. National Security Strategy, for
example, reinforces economic and supply chain security, industrial capacity, technological
leadership, and capital market strength as national priorities. China's latest five-year plan,
meanwhile, includes technological self-reliance, economic stability and the modernization
of core industries among its key pillars. In this environment, value creation is increasingly

shaped by new trade corridors, growing defense spending, expansion of export controls
and industrial policies, and shifting flows of foreign investment."1 These new trade
policies are an impetus for growth that is structural, helping to make for a strong
investment cycle.
Obviously, AI is a big spur for investment that has been facilitated by the new policies
such as accelerated depreciation of capital expenditures (capex). McKinsey Research
designates 18 arenas with cutting-edge industries they believe are driving the global

growth story for the next couple of decades. They note companies headquartered in
the U.S. and China account for about 90% of these arenas' market value today. U.S.
companies account for 75% of the market capitalization within the 18 arenas and
generate about 50% of revenues. Given its leading role in the arenas driving growth
into the future, we believe the U.S. is well poised to outperform as the world moves
into this new technological future. The 18 arenas are part of the economy growing by

several multiples of the old economy segments and help explain why U.S. Equities have a
persistent valuation premium over the rest of the world.
Because of the unusually rapid growth of these new technologies associated with the
singularity, while earnings growth may decelerate from its peak, there are structural
reasons to believe earnings are on a new, higher-growth trajectory and unlikely to revert
fully back to historical norms. The new technologies have much higher margins than the

old economy sectors. The combination of faster growth and higher margins bodes well
for the equity market as these new arenas of future economic growth become a bigger
part of the U.S. and global economies.
The midterms have changed views about the relative performance of some segments
of the economy. For example, strong sentiment to slow the buildout of AI data centers
was dramatically evident in the election results. Beneficiaries of the buildout are being

revalued to reflect this slower growth. Disapproval of the Iran War was also registered
in the results. Energy prices have dropped on hopes for a quicker end to the war. This in
turn has lowered inflation expectations and interest rates. Overall, while the midterms
have remixed relative performance between sectors they have not changed the outlook
for continued solid growth and an ongoing bull market in equities.
1 McKinsey Quarterly, Q3 2026, page 52.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
China, the WTO and the Great Convergence No One Expected
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Twenty-five years ago this December, the West made one of the biggest economic bets
of the modern era: It brought China into the WTO, convinced that membership would
transform the communist giant into a more open and market-oriented economy.
The logic seemed impeccable at the time. The Soviet Union had long been vanquished.

American capitalism appeared triumphant. Free trade, deregulation, privatization and
unfettered cross-border flows of goods, services, capital and labor were widely viewed as
essential ingredients of economic prosperity. Globalization ruled.
Fold China into the rules-based system, the thinking went, and economic liberalization
would gradually pull Beijing toward Western norms. China would become a willing
participant in the U.S.-led global economic order. The world would change China, in

other words.
Not quite. At the dawn of the 25th anniversary of China’s WTO accession, the great irony is
not how China became more like the West. It is how the West became more like China.
In the U.S., and across Europe and Asia, governments are increasingly embracing the
elements of China’s economic playbook, which range from state subsidies to the promotion
of national champions to strategic industrial policies, among other initiatives antithetical to

the global economic norms that prevailed when China joined the WTO.
The deployment of trade tariffs, the subsidization of semiconductor production, the
promotion of domestic manufacturing, and the screening of foreign investment are no
longer hallmarks of China alone. They are increasingly features of economic policymaking
in the U.S., the one-time champion of globalization. The European Union, Japan, South
Korea and other nations that once carried the flag for free trade and open markets have

moved much in the same direction. Even the World Bank—a chief architect and guardian of
Western-led globalization—has come around to blessing the merits of industrial policy, a
strategy central to China’s economic ascent.
To be sure, Washington has not embraced China’s economic model wholesale. Neither have
Brussels, Tokyo or Seoul. The political, economic and institutional differences between
these systems and China’s remain vast. But the direction—set by China—is unmistakable.

It was not supposed to be this way. To gain admission to the WTO, China made significant
concessions over 15 years of negotiations. Beijing slashed tariffs on industrial goods and
agricultural products. Various sectors—manufacturing, distribution, telecommunications,
banking and autos—were gradually opened to foreign firms. Commercial laws and regulations
were amended or written, including rules governing intellectual property rights. Nontariff
barriers like quotas, import licenses, dual pricing and opaque industry standards were either

eliminated or phased out. State-owned enterprises were reformed and restructured.
These concessions—and more—were taken as signs that China was serious about
becoming a global stakeholder. It stoked optimism among senior U.S. officials that China
was inevitably bound to converge and embrace Western economic norms. And, for a while,
the bet seemed to pay off.
As China lowered trade and investment barriers at the beginning of the century, U.S. goods

exports soared. American firms poured capital into the mainland, attracted by an enormous
labor pool, lower production costs and what would soon become one of the largest
consumer markets in the world.
China’s massive labor pool-cum-ballooning middle class paid off handsomely for Corporate
America. U.S. affiliate income earned in China rose from just $1.6 billion (B) in 2001 to
nearly $9B by 2010, making China one of the most profitable markets in the world for U.S.

goods and services. American consumers, meanwhile, enjoyed the benefits of low-cost
China imports. And debt-laden America benefited as China recycled its excess savings into
U.S. Treasurys, helping to grease the financial wheels of America.
Investment Implications
We expect China will continue to rely
on exports and investment to drive
growth amid weak consumption and
domestic demand. Manufacturing-
heavy economies like Germany
remain particularly exposed,

reinforcing our neutral stance toward
Europe in portfolios.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But just as WTO accession helped boost China’s imports, it also supercharged China’s
export machine. Rather than shrink, as some optimists had predicted, America’s
merchandise trade deficit with China more than doubled between 2001 ($83.2B) and 2005
($202.8B) and continued rising thereafter. As the decade wore on, U.S. manufacturing jobs
and wages came under intense pressure owing to expanding China imports and the shift

in U.S. production to lower-cost China. Entire supply chains migrated across the Pacific,
decimating many manufacturing communities across the U.S.
China began producing and exporting at a scale the world had never experienced. In the
span of just a decade, China’s share of world manufacturing output soared from 8.7%
in 2000 to 18.1% in 2010, when the nation eclipsed the U.S. as the world’s largest
manufacturer of goods. Four years later, China was outproducing all of Europe—and has

never looked back (Exhibit 1A).
Today, China sits at the center of global manufacturing and trade, competing not simply in
textiles, toys and other low-value products but also in more technology-driven industries
like electric vehicles, drones, machinery and clean energy. China now accounts for roughly
28% of global manufacturing, according to the World Bank, well ahead of second place
America (17%). Meanwhile, the nation’s share of world exports—just 4% in 2001—now

stands at 15%, roughly on par with the combined market share of second-place America
and Germany (Exhibit 1B). The world is so awash with China goods that the nation is on
course to post another annual merchandise trade surplus in excess of $1 trillion.
Not surprisingly, China’s grip on world trade has significantly dimmed the brimming
optimism that greeted the nation’s WTO entry a quarter-century ago. Today, the consensus
is that China’s trade surplus has been aided and abetted by nonmarket practices and

policies that include forced technology transfers, currency manipulations, state subsidies
to industry, restrictions on foreign investment in strategic sectors, intellectual property
infringements and resource protectionism. Rather than dismantle its state-led, nonmarket
and mercantilist economy, China has tacked in the other direction, notably under the
leadership of Xi Jinping.
For investors, the implications have been profound. We now live in a world where

governments increasingly decide which industries are strategically indispensable, which
technologies must remain domestically controlled and which companies require public
financing. The “Commanding Heights”—or who controls the economy—have shifted
from the private sector to the public sector. In this world, investment returns are not only
determined by traditional metrics like earnings, the cost of capital and valuations but also
by geopolitics, energy costs, trade protectionism and national security. The world today is

far different from the world when China joined the WTO.
Suffice it to say that the 25th anniversary of China’s entry into the WTO won’t be met
with much celebration in the West. The consensus in 2001 was that the world would
change China. Twenty-five years later, it’s China that has changed the world. It’s the great
convergence no one saw coming.

6 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are we reviewing to gauge the capital investment needed for the proposed Artificial
Intelligence buildout and the return required to cover its costs?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Capex by the 15 largest data center developers are expected to top $1.4 trillion in
2027, which is somewhere between 3.5% and 4% of U.S. GDP. A large portion of the
capital investment is earmarked for compute. The total capacity of gigawatts (GW)

under construction is around 34GW of Informaton Technology capacity, with the U.S.
accounting for approximately 70% of this, according to BloombergNEF. Given the
massive compute investment, we continue to analyze closely the total debt financing
announced, the approximate cost of capital of the capital providers, the associated
revenues needed to match or exceed cost estimates, and the individual credit spreads
(as well as credit default swaps spreads) of the larger hyperscalers and chip makers to

better assess the overall return on investment of the AI buildout.
BloombergNEF estimates, that at the beginning of this year, the highest-rated data
center bonds offered yields below 5%, which is now closer to around 7% today. In
addition, AI-tied firms have raised over $460B in debt financing this year, which is
more than double that from 2025. Finally, according to BloombergNEF and Bloomberg
Intelligence, as of September 18, 2026, it is estimated that the 15 largest publicly listed

data center companies would need to produce $2.5 trillion in revenues in 2032 in order
to achieve a return on invested capital equal to the company-level weighted average cost
of capital. This is about 5 times more than the expected revenue in 2026.
Recently, the concern across the capital markets is that there is not enough financing
capital to go around in order to fund the expected buildout, which is one of the primary
reasons why credit spreads have widened and yields have risen. We view this as a natural

development of such large-scale infrastructure transformations. This is the reason why
we continue to watch the overall health of balance sheets of the builders, the level of
profitability and margins, and the order backlogs/cancellations in the next few years. We
still believe we are closer to the middle of the AI buildout than to the end. Therefore, we
view larger-scale weakness in the higher-quality segments of the AI chain as potential
buying opportunities for investors who are underexposed to these areas.

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: risks mount
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
10:31 November 20 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October, employment falling 43.5k following
September’s 36.4k decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the year have now slowed
to 1.1%yr, materially below current growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the

unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9% despite the participation rate falling
another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

### Page 2

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer momentum in hiring, now we are seeing
evidence of a material increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have remained in
employment to date, but persistent job shedding raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend
in the unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most
certainly before the late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3
increase was skewed towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay outcomes directly
tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements
meanwhile, the most responsive to current and expected labour market conditions, accounted for their
smallest share of a Q3 wage increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour cost

growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident in housing. The Westpac Housing
Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005. Interestingly,
there remains considerable latent demand: almost 13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over
the next year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak sentiment, high interest rates and

expectations of further price declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These factors also
pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for inflation remained the critical theme. The
October FOMC minutes highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs and energy
prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several [members] judg[ing] that price pressures were
increasingly extending beyond industries directly affected by data center construction”. Participants

"assessed that medium- and longer-term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-target inflation is a threat to the status
quo, and perceived risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke throughout the week
also kept the focus on price risks, and called for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again
proposed a 50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at the December and January

meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales
gave further justification for this view this week, the control group surprising to the upside with a 0.6%
gain in October after a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said, housing is under
considerable pressure, pending home sales falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back

at 2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent weakness in this sector will, in time, have
implications for both consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international development of the week. Again, it provided
a call for pro-active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr
and the decline in fixed asset investment broadened outside property, with capital expenditure now

down 8.1% across the economy year-to-date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth; though, even here, momentum softened
in October, annual growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not only activity that

### Page 3

Related articles
remains under pressure, but also wealth, with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective, stimulus needs to affect change in both activity
and wealth and do so in a sustainable manner.

STRONG RESILIENCE TO EXTERNAL SHOCKS
, 
SOLID 
MACROECONOMIC FUNDAMENTALS 
◼ 
 Over the past 
 two years
 , India has demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of external shocks, while its 
macroeconomic fundamentals have remained solid.  
◼ 
 In FY2025/2026  
(ended in March 2026)
 , India was hit by a sharp rise in US tariffs. However, thanks to supportive fiscal 
and monetary policies, 
 real GDP  
grew by 7.8% in FY2026, placing India among the fastest
 -
 growing Asian economies.  
◼ 
 For the current fiscal year, the Indian economy is facing rising energy prices 
 (
due to 
 the conflict in the Middle East and 
increased food prices 
 because 
 of 
an 
unfavourable monsoon
 ) 
as well as 
 monetary tightening. Nevertheless, despite these 
new challenges, real GDP growth is expected to exceed 7%.  
◼ 
 India is currently in a stronger position than it was in 2022 to cope with the new energy shock. The fiscal leeway to support  
the economy has increased with the decline in the fiscal deficit, and inflation is more contained.  
◼ 
 Although 
 rising  
energy prices have put pressure on external accounts, FX reserves 
 remain  
sufficient to cover the country’s 
short
 -
 term external financing needs. 
 Yet, 
downward pressures on the rupee have been 
 significant 
 and 
could persist in the 
short term
 . 
In 
H1 
2026, India’s external accounts withstood the rise in energy prices fairly well
 , notably  
thanks to the sharp increase 
in workers’ remittances. 
 T
he 
widening of the trade deficit was primarily due to the substantial increase in machinery and 
equipment imports 
 while t
 he oil trade deficit increased 
 only 
moderately, as the rise in energy prices was offset by a decline 
in import volumes. 
 However, 
in H2 2026, the oil trade deficit is expected to widen significantly.  
Additionally, large portfolio outflows have exacerbated downward pressure on the rupee, necessitating interventions by 
the central bank in foreign exchange markets and the adoption of regulatory measures to support the currency. Although 
these measures hav
 e led to a rebound in the rupee (at least temporarily), inflationary pressures, volatile portfolio 
investments, and low net foreign direct investments are 
 expected to continue 
 to put pressure on the currency.  
◼ 
 D
ownward pressures on the rupee 
 and 
 rising bond yields will reduce the government's 
 already limited 
 fiscal capacity to 
increase public investment and 
 support economic development. However, this will not undermine its ability to refinance 
its debt, given its safe structure.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector is sufficiently robust to cope with the expected rise in credit risks.

STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINTS

per year

(AI)

◼ 
 Moreover, India’s development remains highly uneven. 
 The middle class is still modest
 . This is explained by a large 
informal economy, heavy concentration of employment in low value
 -
 added sectors such as agriculture, low participation 
rates among women and youth, and limited industrial development. All this, in turn, reflects India’s weak i

◼ 
 India faces two key structural challenges: i

Even with progress in sectors such as smartphones and pharmaceuticals, India’s industrial base remains too small to 
absorb its vast labour force.

u
r market 
 rigidities

 : 
 despite

years.

attractiveness

 Despite very strong growth, India's attractiveness is low relative to the size of its economy. The stock of foreign direct 
investment stood at only 15% of GDP in Q2 2026 compared to 52% of GDP in Vietnam and around 67% of GDP in Thailand 
and Malaysia.

 u

 s

 of goods

 , 
since its exports of AI

 . 
However,

 -

 s

The sectors most likely to benefit from the recent trade agreement with the EU are electronics and semiconductors, 
pharmaceuticals and chemical
 s
, textile and renewable energy technologies. But expanding trade agreements without deep

 tion.

 are 
stalled. Key sticking 
points include market access, particularly 
 in 
India’s sensitive agricultural sector.

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE

Non transfer risk   
India’s external accounts are solid with a low external debt, moderate current account deficit and large FX 
reserves. 
 India’s net debtor position reached only 5.9% of GDP in Q2 2026. In the short term, external accounts 
are expected to deteriorate 
 given 
the rise in energy prices while downward pressures on the rupee should remain 
high.  
◼ 
 External accounts have remained solid 
 despite recent adverse shocks
 . 
 In 2025, external accounts remained solid 
despite the significant increase in US tariffs. In 
 H1 
 2026, they held up well against rising energy prices. Although the 
current account deficit is expected to widen for the current fiscal year as a whole, it should remain contained, and foreign 
exchange reserves should be more than sufficient to cover the co
 untry’s short
 -
 term financing needs. The 
 major  
causes 
for concern are the 
 structurally 
 low level of foreign direct investment (although it is rising) and the increased reliance on 
portfolio investment, which exposes the currency to greater volatility.  
◼ 
 Structural vulnerability to energy price shock
 . 
India is structurally vulnerable to an energy price shock as its 
 oil and gas  
trade deficit is relatively high (2.8% of GDP in 2025). However, in H1 2026, the current account withstood fairly well the 
rise in energy prices especially thanks to the sharp increase in workers’ remittances  
(+0.7 pp to 3.7% of GDP)
 . The trade 
deficit widened significantly but principally because of the strong increase in machinery and equipment imports rather

Systemic credit risk  
The banking sector has strengthened over the past five years
 , 
as illustrated by the improvement in 
 asset  
quality 
and capital buffers. The main sources of credit risks are rising household debt, the rise in energy prices, the 
unfavourable monsoon and monetary tightening.  
◼ 
 The quality of assets has improved significantly. 
 In Q1 2026, the banking sector’s non
 -
 performing loan (NPL) ratio fell 
to a multi
 -
 decade low of 1.8%. For public banks  
(
54.4% of total loans
 )
, the NPL ratio declined to 2.1%.  
◼ 
 Capital buffers are strong. 
 While 
loan loss provisions remained modest at 75.6% of NPLs, capital buffers stayed strong. 
The Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR) rose to 17.7% in Q1 2026, up from 17.4% a year earlier. From April 2027, Indian banks 
will adopt a new Expected Credit Loss (ECL) framewo
 rk, requiring earlier provisioning for potential loan losses. In the 
short term, higher provisioning requirements could push up lending rates and tighten credit conditions 
 while 
 triggering 
capital raising or targeted government recapitalisation. 
 I
n the medium term, the reform could strengthen banking  
sector 
resilience through earlier recognition of credit risks and more robust loss buffers.

^New 
Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds. Near-
term event risks and high earnings expectations prompt us to trim equities to a 
Core holding. The US and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain Overweight gold. Prices are likely to grind higher as near-term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead. In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran. These concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly for the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite the current challenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop. The US economic outlook remains well supported, and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long-term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and AxJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect them to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers. Our positive view on both markets’ 
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India. Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI-related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification. In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment-grade (IG) 
and high-yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3-7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance. We close our EU bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 27) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream, while rotating into alternatives 
helps reduce equity beta exposure. We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub-
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

investment portfolio. The Foundation model showcases a set of allocations focusing on traditional asset classes that are 
accessible to most investors, while the Foundation+ model includes allocations to private assets that may be accessible to 
investors in some jurisdictions, but not others.

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability): We maintain the probability at 30% as AI-driven investment growth continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Key chart  
The US-led AI-focused 
investment boom has lifted 
business confidence. The 
resulting equity market 
rally has boosted US 
household wealth, 
supporting consumption 
 Fig. 8 Robust business confidence amid AI boom; equity rally lifts US household net worth 
Purchasing managers indices; US household net worth as percentage of personal disposable income

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027, as AI-led expansion continues. The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4%, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2.3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026, but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risks 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees core PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2.4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates at 4.0%, after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8 
ECB to hike once more in December, then pause. The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5% y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer-term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy, and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year. Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets, which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non-manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD 133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though, with technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first-home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local-government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a quarterly pace until Q2 2027. Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4%, and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five-year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3% in Q1 2027. Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle in H2 2027. Two government-appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an Underweight view on Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data. With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3-7-year part of the US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We remain Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, 
government bond yields have retraced meaningfully after a 
sharp bear steepening through September and early October. 
We expect real yields to stay elevated over the next six 
months as AI capex and productivity growth continue to drive 
above-trend economic growth. We expect two more 25bps 
rate hikes by June 2027.  
Over the next few months, we expect US government bond 
yields to remain rangebound. The near-term path hinges on 
five factors: 1) the 3 November US mid-term election, where 
a ‘Democratic sweep’ would likely be bond-supportive by 
constraining US President Trump’s fiscal spending agenda, 
while a ‘Republican sweep’ would likely weigh on bonds as 
markets price in further fiscal expansion and a higher term 
premium; 2) Fed communication at upcoming policy meetings; 
3) the US Treasury’s quarterly refunding announcement on 4 
November, where any signal of reduced long-end issuance 
would likely compress long-end yields; 4) softening auction 
demand and repatriation by foreign investors, which add 
uncertainty around demand for US government bonds; and 5) 
the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle East, which is 
keeping oil prices elevated, with a risk of further upside. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect moderating oil prices and the 
fading inflationary impact of tariffs to give the Fed room to 
consider rate cuts. The US 30-year yield, however, is likely to 
stay elevated as fiscal deficit remains substantial next year.  
We expect a final ECB hike to 2.75% by December 2026, then 
a pause in H1 2027, followed by a 25bps cut in Q3 2027. In 
Japan, we expect quarterly pace of BoJ hikes, with one hike 
that takes the policy rate to 1.50% by December 2026 and two 
more in H1 2027, reaching 2.00% before pausing at mid-year.

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10 
DM corporates – Core holding 
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across DM credit. Credit spreads have widened from 
their lows but remain close to historical tights, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight-spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion, 
especially in the IG space, where heavy AI-related issuance 
is weighing on the long end of the curve. AI-driven supply is 
surpassing USD 250bn in 2026 and making up a growing 
share of US IG gross issuance, but there are growing signs 
that hyperscalers are turning to private markets for funding, 
which should help ease public bond market supply pressures.  
We remain selective at the long end of the yield curve and 
favour a duration of 3-7 years, where risk-adjusted carry 
remains most durable. Issuance-driven concessions in 
sectors with strong capex cycles (notably technology, capital 
goods and basic industries) present opportunities for investors 
who can stomach near-term volatility. 
Fig. 12 DM IG spreads have widened from their lows, 
but remain near historically tight levels 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
DM HY spreads have widened to roughly a six-month high. 
Persistent doubts about whether hyperscalers can generate 
adequate returns on massive AI capex investments triggered 
a broad risk asset sell-off. The CCC tier has become the focal 
point of genuine credit concern, with yields hovering near the 
high-15% range and spreads near a four-year high. Market 
focus has shifted to the funding pressures facing AI-related 
neocloud companies. We view this as idiosyncratic for now 
and see a limited impact on the broader HY universe. The 
underlying credit picture is mixed. Leverage is elevated and 
interest coverage is thin, but the broad market has not yet 
shown signs of systemic stress. The US HY default rate 
remains below trend in 2026. 
HY offers an attractive income proposition, with all-in yields of 
over 8%. Its shorter-duration profile, combined with its lower 
sensitivity to changes in the term premium and rate volatility, 
make it an attractive sleeve in the current phase of the cycle. 
We remain attentive to evolving credit rating migration trends 
and sector-specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades 
slow and new issuance clusters around key themes. However, 
healthy issuer fundamentals and stable credit metrics 
continue to support it as a Core holding allocation. 
Fig. 13 DM HY spreads are off the tights 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight 
USD/Core holding for LCY 
We are Overweight EM USD government bonds and retain 
EM LCY government bonds as a Core holding, reflecting a 
balanced perspective across both regional performance and 
currency dynamics. In EM USD sovereign debt, the recent 
spread widening offered an attractive entry point to gain 
exposure to areas benefiting from resilient global growth and 
stable external conditions. Technicals remain favourable, with 
supply being front-loaded and investor positioning remaining 
relatively light compared with historical levels. 
Many EM central banks having tightened earlier and more 
aggressively post-pandemic, preserving credibility and 
maintaining positive real rates. External balances have 
improved, reserve buffers have increased and greater 
reliance on domestic funding reduces external vulnerability. 
Lula’s surprise win in Brazil’s presidential election raises 
political risk. The country’s fiscal trajectory under the incoming 
administration is a key risk to monitor. Fiscal slippage could 
trigger a broader repricing of LATAM risk. In Asia, higher-rated 
issuers continue to offer a valuable buffer during bouts of 
global rate volatility, while select frontier exposures in EMEA 
remain more sensitive to liquidity shifts and changes in risk 
appetite. Index composition also shapes resilience: the EM 
USD benchmark is less exposed to oil price shocks, as 
countries that are not oil importers account for a larger share 
of the benchmark than in the LCY benchmark. We expect 
returns to be primarily driven by carry rather than further 
spread compression.
